WEEK 1 - LITERATURE IN ENGLISH
TOPIC: FOLKTALES – AFRICAN TALES (FEATURES: DIDACTIC,
ENTERTAINMENT, MAGICAL); RECOMMENDED PROSE TEXT
CONTENT:
What are Folktales? Folktales are traditional stories passed down orally from generation to
generation. They are part of the cultural heritage of a people and teach important lessons about
life, values, and society.
Origin of African Folktales:
 Told by elders to children
 Performed around evening fires
 Passed from parents to children
 Part of African oral tradition
 Preserve history, culture, and values
Features of African Folktales:
1. DIDACTIC (Teaching Moral Lessons)
 Every folktale teaches a lesson
 Lessons about good behavior, honesty, kindness, hard work, respect
 Shows consequences of bad behavior
 Examples of lessons:
o "Honesty is the best policy"
o "Greed leads to destruction"
327
o "Pride comes before a fall"
o "Kindness is rewarded"
2. ENTERTAINMENT
 Told to amuse and entertain
 Use of humor and interesting characters
 Exciting plots and adventures
 Call and response patterns
 Songs and chants included
 Dramatic performances
3. MAGICAL ELEMENTS
 Animals talk and behave like humans
 Supernatural powers and events
 Magical transformations
 Use of charms and spells
 Extraordinary happenings
 Mythical creatures
Other Features:
 Simple Language: Easy to understand
 Repetition: Phrases and events repeated for emphasis
 Stock Characters: Tortoise (cunning), Lion (brave), Spider (trickster)
 Formulaic Opening: "Once upon a time..." "Long, long ago..."
 Formulaic Ending: "That is why..." "And that is the end of the story"
328
Common Characters in African Folktales:
 Tortoise (Ijapa): Cunning, clever, greedy, sometimes foolish
 Spider (Anansi): Trickster, wise, clever
 Hare: Quick, witty, small but smart
 Lion: King, brave, powerful
 Hyena: Foolish, greedy, laughable
 Humans: Kings, farmers, hunters, beautiful maidens
Types of African Folktales:
1. Animal Tales: Stories where animals are main characters
2. Trickster Tales: Stories about clever characters who outwit others
3. Origin Tales: Stories that explain why things are the way they are
4. Moral Tales: Stories that directly teach lessons
Example Summary - "Why Tortoise Has a Cracked Shell": Long ago, Tortoise was invited
to a feast in the sky. He borrowed feathers from birds to fly. At the feast, he tricked the birds and
ate all the food. Angry, the birds took back their feathers. Tortoise fell from the sky and his shell
cracked into pieces. That is why tortoise has a cracked shell today.
Lessons: Greed is punished; Trickery has consequences
Elements Present:
 Didactic: Teaches against greed
 Entertainment: Humorous and interesting
 Magical: Animals talk, tortoise flies, feast in the sky
329
Functions of Folktales:
 Educate children about morals and values
 Preserve cultural traditions
 Entertain and bond communities
 Explain natural phenomena
 Pass down history and wisdom
 Develop imagination and creativity
EVALUATION:
1. Define folktales and explain their origin in African culture
2. Explain the three main features of African folktales with examples
3. List five common characters in African folktales and describe their traits
4. Why are folktales important in African society? Give three reasons
5. Using the story "Why Tortoise Has a Cracked Shell," identify the didactic, entertainment,
and magical elements

WEEK 2 - SPEECH WORK
TOPIC: VOWEL SOUND /U:/
The Long Vowel Sound /u:/
 This is a long vowel sound produced with tightly rounded lips
 The tongue is raised high at the back of the mouth
 The sound is tense and prolonged
 It is represented by different spelling patterns
Spelling Patterns for /u:/ Sound:
1. "oo" spelling:
o food, mood, moon, noon, soon, spoon, room, broom, bloom, gloom
o boot, root, shoot, hoot
o cool, pool, tool, school, fool, stool
2. "u" spelling:
o rude, crude, prude
o June, tune, dune
o rule, mule
3. "ew" spelling:
o flew, blew, drew, grew, knew, threw
o chew, stew, few, new
4. "ue" spelling:
o blue, true, glue, clue, due
o continue, pursue, rescue
5. "ui" spelling:
331
o fruit, juice, suit, cruise, bruise
6. "ou" spelling:
o soup, group, troupe
o through, you, youth
Common Words with /u:/ Sound:
 two, who, move, prove, lose, whose, shoes, choose
 flute, parachute, absolute
 wound (injury), tomb, womb
Words to Practice:
 The moon is blue in June
 Choose your food at noon
 The youth flew through the room
 Fruit juice is good for you
 Who knew the truth about the clue?
Exceptions (words that look like /u:/ but are not):
 book, look, cook, good, foot = /u/ (short sound)
 flood, blood = /ʌ/ (different sound)
Articulation Tips:
 Round your lips tightly as if whistling
 Keep the sound long and steady
 Raise the back of your tongue
 The sound should feel tense, not relaxed
332
EVALUATION:
1. Produce the /u:/ sound and give ten words containing this sound
2. Identify the spelling pattern of /u:/ in these words: (a) blue (b) food (c) grew (d) fruit (e)
rule
3. Which of these words contain the /u:/ sound: book, boot, moon, good, juice, foot?
4. Write five sentences using words with the /u:/ sound
5. Explain the difference between /u/ and /u:/ sounds using examples
ASSIGNMENT:
1. List ten words with "oo" spelling that produce the /u:/ sound
2. List five words each with "ew," "ue," and "ui" spellings that produce /u:/ sound
3. Write ten sentences using different words with /u:/ sound
4. Identify words with /u:/ sound in this sentence: "The rude youth threw his blue shoe
through the school room"
5. Practice reading this passage aloud, paying attention to /u:/ sounds: "In June, the new
student knew the rules. He would choose fruit juice at noon and move to his room to do
his school work."
333
WEEK 2 - GRAMMAR
TOPIC: TENSES – SIMPLE PRESENT TENSE
What is the Simple Present Tense? The Simple Present Tense is used to describe actions that
happen regularly, facts, habits, general truths, and permanent situations.
Formation of Simple Present Tense:
For Regular Verbs:
 I/You/We/They + base form of verb
o I play, You eat, We run, They sing
 He/She/It + base form + s/es
o He plays, She eats, It runs, Mary sings
Rules for Adding -s or -es:
1. Add -s to most verbs:
o work → works, play → plays, eat → eats, sleep → sleeps
2. Add -es to verbs ending in -s, -sh, -ch, -x, -z, -o:
o wash → washes, teach → teaches, go → goes, fix → fixes, buzz → buzzes
3. For verbs ending in consonant + y, change y to i and add -es:
o cry → cries, fly → flies, try → tries, study → studies
4. For verbs ending in vowel + y, just add -s:
o play → plays, enjoy → enjoys, say → says
Uses of Simple Present Tense:
1. Habits and Routines:
o I wake up at 6 AM every day
334
o She brushes her teeth twice daily
o They go to church on Sundays
2. General Truths and Facts:
o The sun rises in the east
o Water boils at 100 degrees Celsius
o Birds fly in the sky
o Nigeria is in West Africa
3. Permanent Situations:
o I live in Lagos
o My father works in a bank
o She speaks three languages
4. Scheduled Events:
o The train leaves at 8 PM
o School starts at 7:30 AM
o The match begins at 4 PM
Negative Form:
 I/You/We/They + do not (don't) + base verb
o I don't like rice
o They don't play football
 He/She/It + does not (doesn't) + base verb
o He doesn't eat meat
o She doesn't live here
Question Form:
335
 Do + I/you/we/they + base verb?
o Do you speak English?
o Do they go to school?
 Does + he/she/it + base verb?
o Does he play tennis?
o Does she know you?
Time Expressions Used with Simple Present:
 Always, usually, often, sometimes, rarely, never
 Every day/week/month/year
 On Mondays/Tuesdays, etc.
 In the morning/afternoon/evening
 At night
Examples:
 I study English every day
 My mother cooks delicious food
 The teacher explains the lesson clearly
 Dogs bark at strangers
 He doesn't smoke
 Do you understand the question?
Common Mistakes to Avoid:
 ❌ He go to school (Correct: He goes to school)
 ❌ She don't like it (Correct: She doesn't like it)
336
 ❌ Do he play? (Correct: Does he play?)
 ❌ The sun rise in the east (Correct: The sun rises in the east)
EVALUATION:
1. Define Simple Present Tense and state four uses with examples
2. Write the correct form of these verbs in Simple Present for "He/She": (a) wash (b) cry (c)
go (d) study (e) play
3. Change to negative: (a) She likes mangoes (b) They speak French (c) He goes to work
daily
4. Change to questions: (a) You read books (b) She knows the answer (c) They live in
Abuja
5. Fill in the blanks with the correct form: (a) The baby _____ (cry) every night (b) I _____
(not/eat) meat (c) _____ he _____ (go) to school? (d) Birds _____ (fly) (e) She _____
(teach) Mathematics
ASSIGNMENT:
1. Write ten sentences about your daily routine using Simple Present Tense
2. Write five general truths or facts using Simple Present Tense
3. Change these sentences to negative form: (a) I play football (b) She watches TV (c) They
go to church (d) He studies hard (e) We eat rice
4. Change these sentences to question form: (a) You speak English (b) He likes reading (c)
They live here (d) She knows me (e) It works well
5. Correct the errors in these sentences: (a) He don't go to school (b) She study every day (c)
Do she like it? (d) They goes home (e) I doesn't know
337
WEEK 2 - READING AND COMPREHENSION
TOPIC: ASKING PROJECTION QUESTIONS; VOCABULARY – THE LIBRARY
PART A: ASKING PROJECTION QUESTIONS
What are Projection Questions? Projection questions require readers to predict, imagine, or
extend beyond the given text. They ask "What might happen next?" or "What would happen
if...?"
Types of Projection Questions:
1. Prediction Questions:
o What do you think will happen next?
o How will the story end?
o What might the character do?
2. Hypothetical Questions:
o What would happen if...?
o How would you feel if...?
o What would you do in this situation?
3. Extension Questions:
o What might happen after the story ends?
o How could this situation be different?
o What other solutions are possible?
4. Personal Connection Questions:
o Have you experienced something similar?
o How would you react in this situation?
o What would you do differently?
338
How to Answer Projection Questions:
 Base predictions on clues in the text
 Use logical reasoning
 Consider character traits and motivations
 Think about cause and effect
 Use your imagination while staying realistic
 Support your answer with evidence from the passage
Sample Passage: "Tunde had studied for only one hour the night before the examination. His
friends had studied for weeks. As he sat in the examination hall looking at the questions, his
hands began to shake. The questions looked unfamiliar."
Projection Questions & Answers:
1. What do you think will happen to Tunde in the exam?
o Answer: Tunde will likely perform poorly because he didn't prepare adequately,
unlike his friends who studied for weeks.
2. What might Tunde do differently next time?
o Answer: He might start studying earlier and prepare more thoroughly for his
examinations.
3. What would you do if you were in Tunde's situation?
o Answer: I would try to stay calm, answer the questions I know, and learn from
this experience to prepare better next time.
Signal Words for Projection Questions:
 What might/could/would...?
 Predict, imagine, suppose
 If..., then...?
339
 What do you think...?
 How would...?
PART B: VOCABULARY – THE LIBRARY
What is a Library? A library is a place where books, magazines, newspapers, and other reading
materials are kept for people to read, borrow, or reference.
Library Vocabulary:
Places in the Library:
 Reading room: Area for reading silently
 Reference section: Books that cannot be borrowed
 Circulation desk: Where books are borrowed and returned
 Catalogue room: Where book records are kept
 Periodicals section: Newspapers and magazines
 Children's section: Books for young readers
 Study carrel: Individual study space
People in the Library:
 Librarian: Person who manages the library
 Library assistant: Helps the librarian
 Borrower/Patron: Person who uses the library
 Reader: Person reading in the library
Library Materials:
 Fiction: Imaginative stories (novels, short stories)
 Non-fiction: Factual books (biographies, textbooks)
340
 Reference books: Dictionaries, encyclopedias, atlases
 Periodicals: Newspapers, magazines, journals
 Audiovisual materials: CDs, DVDs, tapes
 E-books: Digital books
Library Activities:
 Borrow: Take books home temporarily
 Return: Bring books back
 Renew: Extend borrowing period
 Reserve: Book a material in advance
 Browse: Look through books casually
 Research: Search for information
 Catalogue: List of all library materials
Library Rules:
 Maintain silence
 Handle books with care
 Return books on time
 No eating or drinking
 Replace books properly
 Show library card when borrowing
 Pay fines for late returns
 No tearing or marking books
Parts of a Book:
 Cover: Outside protection
341
 Spine: Back edge of book
 Title page: Page showing book title and author
 Table of contents: List of chapters
 Index: Alphabetical list of topics
 Bibliography: List of references
 Glossary: Dictionary of terms used
 Appendix: Additional information
Library Card Information:
 Name of borrower
 Registration number
 Date of issue
 Expiry date
 Library rules
EVALUATION:
1. Explain what projection questions are and give three examples
2. Read this passage: "Ada found a wallet on the street with money inside but no
identification." Ask three projection questions based on this passage
3. Define a library and state five items you can find there
4. Explain the difference between fiction and non-fiction books with examples
5. List five library rules and explain why they are important
ASSIGNMENT:
342
1. Write a short paragraph (100 words) describing your school library
2. List ten vocabulary words related to the library and use each in a sentence
3. Read this passage and answer the projection questions that follow: "Chioma had saved
money for three months to buy a new school bag. On her way to the shop, she saw a little
girl crying because she had no money for food." Questions: (a) What do you think
Chioma will do? (b) What would you do in her situation? (c) What might happen after?
4. Draw and label a simple diagram showing five sections of a library
5. Explain the role of a librarian in five sentences

1. Request Letters:
o Permission for absence

344

WEEK 2 - LITERATURE IN ENGLISH
TOPIC: INTRODUCTION TO POETRY – DEFINITION, FEATURES (CONCISE,
UNIQUE, ETC.)
CONTENT:
What is Poetry? Poetry is a type of literature that uses carefully chosen words, rhythm, and
imagery to express feelings, ideas, and experiences in a creative and artistic way. It appeals to
emotions and imagination.
Differences Between Poetry and Prose:
Poetry:
 Written in lines and stanzas
 Uses rhythm and sometimes rhyme
 More compact and concentrated
 Uses figurative language heavily
 Often emotional and imaginative
 Can be read aloud with musical quality
Prose:
 Written in sentences and paragraphs
 No specific rhythm pattern
 More straightforward
 Less figurative language
 Can be fiction or non-fiction
352
 Everyday language
Features of Poetry:
1. CONCISE (Brief and Compact)
 Poetry uses few words to express big ideas
 Every word is carefully chosen
 No unnecessary words
 Compact expression of thoughts and feelings
 Example: "The sun smiles" (instead of "The sun is shining brightly and looks happy")
2. UNIQUE/CREATIVE USE OF LANGUAGE
 Poets use words in special ways
 Creative word arrangements
 Fresh and original expressions
 Unexpected word combinations
 Plays with meaning and sound
3. LINES AND STANZAS
 Line: A single row of words in a poem
 Stanza: A group of lines forming a unit (like a paragraph)
 Poems are divided into stanzas, not paragraphs
 Line breaks are meaningful
4. RHYTHM AND METER
 Rhythm: Pattern of stressed and unstressed syllables
 Creates musical quality
353
 Makes poetry pleasant to hear
 Can be regular or free
5. RHYME
 Rhyme: Similar sounds at the end of lines
 Examples: cat/hat, moon/soon, day/way
 Not all poems rhyme
 Rhyme scheme: Pattern of rhyme (AABB, ABAB, etc.)
6. FIGURATIVE LANGUAGE
 Simile: Comparison using "like" or "as" (Her smile is like sunshine)
 Metaphor: Direct comparison (Her smile is sunshine)
 Personification: Giving human qualities to non-human things (The wind whispered)
 Imagery: Descriptive language appealing to senses
7. EMOTIONAL AND IMAGINATIVE
 Expresses feelings (love, joy, sadness, anger)
 Appeals to imagination
 Creates mental pictures
 Touches the heart
8. THEME
 Central message or idea
 What the poem is about
 Common themes: love, nature, death, freedom, childhood
Types of Poetry:
354
1. Narrative Poetry:
 Tells a story
 Has characters and plot
 Example: Epics, ballads
2. Lyric Poetry:
 Expresses personal feelings
 Musical quality
 Example: Odes, sonnets
3. Descriptive Poetry:
 Describes scenes, people, things
 Rich in imagery
4. Free Verse:
 No regular rhythm or rhyme
 Modern style
 Flexible form
Sample Poem:
THE RAIN
By Anonymous
The rain falls softly on the roof,
Pitter-patter, gentle and aloof,
Washing the earth with loving care,
355
Bringing freshness everywhere.
Plants dance in joyful celebration,
Children run in happy recreation,
The rain brings life to all around,
What a beautiful, soothing sound!
Analysis:
 Concise: Uses simple words to paint a picture
 Lines and Stanzas: Two stanzas of four lines each
 Rhyme: roof/aloof, care/everywhere (AABB pattern)
 Rhythm: Regular beat throughout
 Figurative Language: "Plants dance" (personification), "washing the earth with loving
care" (personification)
 Imagery: We can hear and see the rain
 Theme: Rain brings life and joy
How to Read Poetry:
 Read slowly
 Pay attention to word choice
 Notice rhythm and sound
 Look for imagery and figurative language
 Think about feelings and emotions
356
 Read aloud to hear the musical quality
 Don't worry if you don't understand everything at first
Elements to Look for When Reading Poems:
 Title (gives clues about the poem)
 Speaker (who is talking?)
 Tone (mood of the poem)
 Setting (where and when?)
 Theme (main message)
 Poetic devices used
EVALUATION:
1. Define poetry and state three ways it differs from prose
2. Explain five features of poetry with examples
3. What is the difference between a line and a stanza in poetry?
4. Identify the figurative language in these expressions: (a) The stars danced in the sky (b)
Her voice is as sweet as honey (c) Time is money
5. Read the sample poem "The Rain" and identify: (a) the rhyme scheme (b) one example of
personification (c) the theme
ASSIGNMENT:
1. Write a simple four-line poem about your school with a clear rhyme scheme
357
2. Define these poetic terms with examples: (a) simile (b) metaphor (c) personification (d)
imagery (e) stanza
3. Identify three features of poetry in any poem you have read
4. Explain why poetry is described as "concise" and "unique"
5. State the title, author, and brief summary (5 sentences) of your recommended prose text
for this term
358
WEEK 3 - SPEECH WORK
TOPIC: VOWEL SOUND /ɑ:/
The Long Vowel Sound /ɑ:/
 This is a long, open vowel sound produced at the back of the mouth
 The mouth is wide open
 The tongue is low and flat
 The lips are not rounded
 It is similar to the sound a doctor asks you to make when examining your throat: "Ahhh"
Spelling Patterns for /ɑ:/ Sound:
1. "ar" spelling (most common):
 car, far, bar, jar, tar, star, scar
 arm, farm, harm, charm, alarm
 art, part, cart, start, heart, smart
 dark, park, mark, bark, shark
 card, hard, yard, guard, regard
 March, arch, starch
 large, charge, barge
2. "a" spelling (before certain consonants):
 father, rather
 bath, path, class, grass, pass, glass, task, ask, cast, fast, last, past, master
3. "al" spelling:
 calm, palm, balm, psalm
 half, calf
359
4. "ear" spelling:
 heart
5. "au/a" spelling (rare):
 aunt, laugh
British vs. American Pronunciation: Note: Some words have /ɑ:/ in British English but /æ/ in
American English:
 bath, path, grass, class, dance, chance, plant
Common Words with /ɑ:/ Sound:
 afternoon, army, argue, article, artist
 banana (second 'a'), tomato (second 'a')
 barber, carpet, garden, market, party
 carve, starve, marble
Practice Sentences:
 The car is parked in the dark yard
 My father is a smart artist
 The shark swims far in the large water
 Martha's heart was calm at the party
 The guard marched past the farm
Words That Look Similar But Sound Different:
 war, warm, ward = /ɔ:/ sound (NOT /ɑ:/)
 carry, marry, Harry = /æ/ sound (NOT /ɑ:/)
360
Articulation Tips:
 Open your mouth wide
 Keep tongue flat and low
 Don't round your lips
 Make the sound long and clear
 Think of the sound when you're at the doctor
Practice Pairs:
 had /æ/ - hard /ɑ:/
 cap /æ/ - carp /ɑ:/
 mad /æ/ - marred /ɑ:/
 cat /æ/ - cart /ɑ:/
EVALUATION:
1. Produce the /ɑ:/ sound correctly and explain how it is articulated
2. Give ten words containing the /ɑ:/ sound using "ar" spelling
3. Identify which words contain /ɑ:/ sound: (a) car (b) carry (c) dark (d) war (e) farm (f)
warm (g) part (h) party
4. Write the /ɑ:/ sound words from this sentence: "The farmer's smart daughter walked to
the large market in the dark"
5. Explain the difference in pronunciation between: (a) had/hard (b) cat/cart (c) pat/part
ASSIGNMENT:
1. List fifteen words with "ar" spelling that contain the /ɑ:/ sound
361
2. List five words each with "a" and "al" spellings that produce the /ɑ:/ sound
3. Write ten sentences using words with /ɑ:/ sound, underlining the words
4. Read aloud and practice: "Martha's father is a barber at the market. He starts work before
dark and parks his car in the yard"
5. Identify and correct the pronunciation errors in these words if mispronounced with wrong
vowel sounds: farm, warm, class, carry, march
362
WEEK 3 - GRAMMAR
TOPIC: TENSES – SIMPLE PAST TENSE
What is the Simple Past Tense? The Simple Past Tense is used to describe actions that
happened and were completed in the past. It shows that something started and finished at a
specific time in the past.
Formation of Simple Past Tense:
Regular Verbs: Base form + ed
Rules for Adding -ed:
1. Most verbs - add -ed:
 walk → walked
 play → played
 talk → talked
 work → worked
 listen → listened
2. Verbs ending in -e - add only -d:
 live → lived
 move → moved
 dance → danced
 arrive → arrived
 hope → hoped
3. Verbs ending in consonant + y - change y to i and add -ed:
 cry → cried
 study → studied
363
 try → tried
 carry → carried
 worry → worried
4. Verbs ending in vowel + y - just add -ed:
 play → played
 enjoy → enjoyed
 stay → stayed
 pray → prayed
5. One-syllable verbs ending in consonant-vowel-consonant - double the final consonant
and add -ed:
 stop → stopped
 plan → planned
 drop → dropped
 shop → shopped
 clap → clapped
Irregular Verbs: These verbs do not follow the -ed rule. They have special past tense forms that
must be memorized:
 go → went
 come → came
 see → saw
 eat → ate
 drink → drank
364
 run → ran
 sit → sat
 stand → stood
 write → wrote
 read → read (pronunciation changes)
 buy → bought
 bring → brought
 teach → taught
 catch → caught
 think → thought
 take → took
 make → made
 give → gave
 get → got
 have/has → had
 do/does → did
 is/am/are → was/were
 say → said
 tell → told
 sleep → slept
 leave → left
 meet → met
 find → found
365
 lose → lost
 break → broke
 speak → spoke
 drive → drove
 fly → flew
 know → knew
 grow → grew
 begin → began
 sing → sang
 swim → swam
 drink → drank
Uses of Simple Past Tense:
1. Completed actions in the past:
 I visited my grandmother yesterday
 She finished her homework last night
 They traveled to Abuja last month
2. Past habits (no longer true):
 I lived in Lagos when I was young
 He played football every weekend
 We went to the beach every summer
3. Series of completed actions:
 I woke up, brushed my teeth, and went to school
366
 She entered the room, greeted everyone, and sat down
Negative Form:
 Subject + did not (didn't) + base verb
o I didn't go to school yesterday
o She didn't eat lunch
o They didn't see the movie
o He didn't finish his work
Note: Use the base form of the verb after "didn't," not the past tense.
 ✓ I didn't go (Correct)
 ✗ I didn't went (Wrong)
Question Form:
 Did + subject + base verb...?
o Did you see him?
o Did she finish the book?
o Did they arrive on time?
o Did he play football?
Time Expressions Used with Simple Past:
 Yesterday
 Last (night/week/month/year/Monday, etc.)
 Ago (two days ago, a week ago)
367
 In 2020, in 1999 (specific past years)
 When I was young
 During my childhood
 This morning (if morning is over)
 Just now
Examples:
 I played football yesterday
 She visited the museum last week
 They lived in Kano two years ago
 My father bought a car in 2023
 We didn't go to church last Sunday
 Did you see the accident?
 The teacher taught us English this morning
 I met my friend at the market
Common Mistakes to Avoid:
 ✗ I goed to school (Correct: I went)
 ✗ She didn't came (Correct: She didn't come)
 ✗ Did you went? (Correct: Did you go?)
 ✗ He don't go yesterday (Correct: He didn't go)
 ✗ They was happy (Correct: They were happy)
368
EVALUATION:
1. Define Simple Past Tense and state three uses with examples
2. Write the past tense form of these verbs: (a) study (b) run (c) stop (d) enjoy (e) see (f)
teach (g) buy (h) carry (i) write (j) have
3. Change to Simple Past Tense: (a) I go to school (b) She eats rice (c) They play football (d)
He reads books (e) We are happy
4. Change to negative: (a) I saw him yesterday (b) She came to school (c) They finished the
work (d) He bought a new book (e) We went to the market
5. Change to questions: (a) You visited your uncle (b) She cooked rice yesterday (c) They
arrived early (d) He played well (e) It rained last night
ASSIGNMENT:
1. Write twenty irregular verbs and their past tense forms
2. Write a paragraph (10 sentences) about what you did last Sunday using Simple Past
Tense
3. Fill in the blanks with the correct past tense form: (a) I _____ (go) to the market
yesterday (b) She _____ (not/come) to school last week (c) They _____ (play) football
last Saturday (d) _____ you _____ (see) the accident? (e) We _____ (be) happy at the
party
4. Correct the errors: (a) I goed home (b) She didn't came (c) Did he went? (d) They was
late (e) He don't finish yesterday
5. Change this passage from Present to Past Tense: "I wake up early. I brush my teeth and
take my bath. I eat breakfast and go to school. I meet my friends and we play together."

WEEK 3 - READING AND COMPREHENSION
TOPIC: ASKING RECALL QUESTIONS; VOCABULARY – MARRIAGE-RELATED
WORDS
PART A: ASKING RECALL QUESTIONS
What are Recall Questions? Recall questions are questions that require readers to remember
and retrieve specific information directly stated in the passage. The answers are found word-forword
or very clearly in the text.
Characteristics of Recall Questions:
 Answers are explicitly stated in the text
 No inference or interpretation needed
 Test your memory of what you read
 Have clear, definite answers
 Focus on facts, details, and specific information
Types of Recall Questions:
1. Who Questions:
 Who is the main character?
 Who did...?
 Who said...?
2. What Questions:
 What happened?
 What did the character do?
 What is the name of...?
370
3. When Questions:
 When did the event occur?
 When did the character...?
4. Where Questions:
 Where did the story take place?
 Where was...?
5. How Many/How Much Questions:
 How many people...?
 How much did it cost?
6. Why Questions (for stated reasons):
 Why did he go? (if reason is given)
Signal Words in Recall Questions:
 Name, list, identify, state
 According to the passage
 The passage states/says
 What is the name of...?
 How many...?
 When exactly...?
How to Answer Recall Questions:
371
Step 1: Read the question carefully Step 2: Identify key words in the question Step 3: Scan the
passage for those key words Step 4: Read the relevant section carefully Step 5: Write the answer
using words from the passage Step 6: Check that your answer directly addresses the question
Sample Passage:
"Amina Bello is a 12-year-old student at Government Secondary School, Lagos. She lives at 23
Benson Street with her parents and two younger brothers. Every morning, she wakes up at 5:30
AM to prepare for school. Her favorite subject is Mathematics, and she wants to become an
engineer. Last term, she scored 95% in her Mathematics examination and received the Best
Student Award in her class, JSS 1B."
Recall Questions and Answers:
1. What is the full name of the student?
o Answer: Amina Bello
2. Where does Amina live?
o Answer: 23 Benson Street (or: She lives at 23 Benson Street with her parents)
3. What time does she wake up every morning?
o Answer: 5:30 AM
4. What is her favorite subject?
o Answer: Mathematics
5. What percentage did she score in Mathematics last term?
o Answer: 95%
6. How many brothers does she have?
o Answer: Two younger brothers
7. What does she want to become?
o Answer: An engineer
372
8. Which class is she in?
o Answer: JSS 1B
Difference Between Recall and Other Question Types:
Recall Question: What award did Amina receive? (Answer is in the text: Best Student Award)
Inference Question: Why do you think Amina received the award? (Requires reasoning:
Because she works hard and scored high marks)
Projection Question: What might Amina become in the future? (Requires prediction: She might
become a successful engineer)
PART B: VOCABULARY – MARRIAGE-RELATED WORDS
Marriage Vocabulary:
People Involved:
 Bride: The woman getting married
 Groom/Bridegroom: The man getting married
 Couple: The bride and groom together
 Spouse: Husband or wife
 Husband: Married man
 Wife: Married woman
 Fiancé: Man engaged to be married
 Fiancée: Woman engaged to be married
 Best man: Groom's chief attendant
 Maid of honor/Chief bridesmaid: Bride's chief attendant
 Bridesmaids: Female attendants to the bride
373
 Groomsmen: Male attendants to the groom
 Parents-in-law: Parents of your spouse (father-in-law, mother-in-law)
 Officiating minister/Pastor/Imam: Person conducting the ceremony
Types of Marriage:
 Monogamy: Marriage to one person
 Polygamy: Marriage to more than one person
 Civil marriage: Legal marriage conducted by government officials
 Religious marriage: Marriage conducted by religious leaders
 Traditional marriage: Marriage according to cultural customs
 Court wedding: Marriage at the court/registry
Marriage Events and Ceremonies:
 Engagement: Formal agreement to marry
 Introduction: When families meet formally
 Bridal shower: Party for the bride before wedding
 Bachelor party: Party for the groom before wedding
 Wedding ceremony: The marriage event
 Wedding reception: Celebration after the ceremony
 Honeymoon: Holiday taken by newlyweds after wedding
Marriage Items and Symbols:
 Wedding ring: Ring exchanged during marriage
 Engagement ring: Ring given during engagement
 Wedding gown/dress: Special dress worn by bride
 Suit/Tuxedo: Formal wear for groom
374
 Veil: Cloth covering bride's face
 Bouquet: Bunch of flowers carried by bride
 Wedding cake: Special cake for celebration
 Wedding invitation: Card inviting people to wedding
Marriage Expressions and Actions:
 Propose: To ask someone to marry you
 Accept proposal: To agree to marry someone
 Exchange vows: To make marriage promises
 Walk down the aisle: Bride's walk to the altar
 Give away the bride: Father presenting bride to groom
 Say "I do": Accept marriage vows
 Kiss the bride: Traditional kiss after vows
 Throw bouquet: Bride tosses flowers to unmarried women
 Cut the cake: Couple cuts wedding cake together
 First dance: Couple's first dance as married people
Marriage-Related Verbs:
 Marry, wed, propose, engage
 Divorce, separate (end of marriage)
 Celebrate, honor, cherish
Marriage Documents:
 Marriage certificate: Official document of marriage
 Marriage license: Permission to marry
 Wedding program: Order of ceremony events
375
Traditional Marriage Terms (African Context):
 Bride price/Dowry: Payment to bride's family
 Traditional attire: Cultural clothing for ceremony
 Wine carrying: Traditional ceremony element
 Breaking of kola nut: Traditional ritual
EVALUATION:

2. Read this passage and answer the recall questions: "Mr. and Mrs. Johnson got married on

years old) and a girl named Grace (3 years old). They live in Abuja." Questions: (a)
When did Mr. and Mrs. Johnson get married? (b) Where did they get married? (c) How

4. List five items or symbols associated with weddings

bride (e) vows
ASSIGNMENT:

2. Match these people to their roles: (a) Best man (b) Officiating minister (c) Maid of honor
(d) Groom (e) Bride
376
3. Explain the difference between: (a) engagement and wedding (b) bride and groom (c)
civil and traditional marriage
4. List ten marriage-related verbs and use each in a sentence

She accepted, and they got engaged. Their wedding took place six months later at the
National Mosque. Two hundred guests attended the reception at Sheraton Hotel."

WEEK 3 - COMPOSITION
TOPIC: GUIDED COMPOSITION – THE FEDERAL ROAD SAFETY COMMISSION
(FRSC)
What is Guided Composition? Guided composition is a writing exercise where you are given
specific information, points, or guidelines to help you write. You organize the given information
into complete sentences and paragraphs.
THE FEDERAL ROAD SAFETY COMMISSION (FRSC)
Background Information:
What is FRSC? The Federal Road Safety Commission (FRSC) is a Nigerian government
agency responsible for road safety management, enforcement of traffic rules, and reduction of
road accidents.
Key Information About FRSC:
1. Establishment:
 Created by Decree Number 45 of 1988
 Became operational on February 18, 1988
 Founded to address increasing road accidents in Nigeria
2. Motto and Logo:
 Motto: "Safety First"
 Logo: Features the map of Nigeria in green and white
 Uniform: White and green colors representing peace and safety
3. Functions/Duties:
 Prevent and reduce road traffic accidents
 Clear obstruction on highways
 Educate motorists and road users on safety
378
 Enforce traffic rules and regulations
 Issue driver's licenses
 Conduct driving tests
 Register vehicles
 Provide first aid and rescue during accidents
 Patrol highways and roads
 Recommend road safety measures to government
4. Road Safety Rules Enforced by FRSC:
 Use of seat belts (mandatory for all)
 Speed limits must be obeyed
 No use of phone while driving
 Valid driver's license required
 Vehicle registration and insurance
 No overloading of vehicles
 No drinking and driving
 Proper use of road signs
 Use of child restraints
 Working lights and indicators
5. Safety Tips from FRSC:
 Obey all traffic signs and signals
 Maintain safe speed
 Keep safe distance from other vehicles
 Be alert and avoid distractions
379
 Wear seat belts always
 Don't drink and drive
 Ensure your vehicle is roadworthy
 Use pedestrian crossings when walking
 Be visible at night (wear reflective clothing)
6. Penalties for Traffic Offenses:
 Fines for various violations
 Impounding of vehicles
 Suspension of driver's license
 Court prosecution for serious offenses
7. Special Operations:
 Ember months (September-December) special patrols
 Public holiday operations
 School resumption traffic management
 Accident-prone area monitoring
8. FRSC Offices:
 Headquarters in Abuja
 Zonal and state commands
 Unit commands in local areas
 Mobile patrol teams
GUIDED POINTS FOR WRITING:
Paragraph 1 - Introduction:
380
 Name of the organization
 When it was established
 Why it was created
 Its motto
Paragraph 2 - Functions:
 Main responsibilities
 Traffic rules enforcement
 Licensing and registration
 Educational activities
Paragraph 3 - Road Safety Rules:
 Important traffic rules
 Safety measures for drivers
 Safety tips for pedestrians
Paragraph 4 - Conclusion:
 Importance of FRSC
 Appeal for cooperation
 Benefits of obeying traffic rules
SAMPLE GUIDED COMPOSITION:
THE FEDERAL ROAD SAFETY COMMISSION
The Federal Road Safety Commission, popularly known as FRSC, is a Nigerian government
agency responsible for ensuring safety on our roads. It was established by Decree Number 45 of
1988 and became operational on February 18, 1988. The commission was created to address the
increasing number of road accidents and deaths on Nigerian roads. Its motto is "Safety First,"
which reminds everyone that nothing is more important than staying safe on the road.
381
The FRSC has many important functions. It prevents and reduces road traffic accidents through
enforcement of traffic rules and public education. The commission issues driver's licenses to
qualified drivers and registers vehicles. FRSC officers patrol highways and roads to ensure
motorists obey traffic laws. They also provide first aid and rescue services during accidents, clear
obstructions on highways, and educate motorists and other road users about safe driving
practices. Additionally, they make recommendations to the government on road safety measures.
There are many important road safety rules that the FRSC enforces. All drivers and passengers
must wear seat belts while in a moving vehicle. Drivers must not use mobile phones while
driving, as this causes distractions. Every driver must have a valid driver's license and ensure
their vehicle is properly registered and insured. Speed limits must be obeyed at all times.
Vehicles should not be overloaded beyond their capacity. Drinking and driving is strictly
prohibited as it impairs judgment. All vehicle lights, indicators, and safety features must be in
good working condition.
The Federal Road Safety Commission plays a vital role in protecting lives and property on
Nigerian roads. Everyone must cooperate with FRSC officers and obey all traffic rules and
regulations. By following road safety measures, we can reduce accidents and make our roads
safer for all users. Remember, safety first!
Writing Tips:
 Use formal language
 Organize information logically
 Write in complete sentences
 Use proper paragraphing
 Connect ideas smoothly
 Include relevant details
 Stay focused on the topic
 Proofread your work
Useful Linking Words:
382
 Additionally, furthermore, moreover (to add information)
 Therefore, thus, consequently (to show result)
 However, nevertheless, although (to contrast)
 Firstly, secondly, finally (to list points)
 For example, for instance (to give examples)
EVALUATION:
1. When was the Federal Road Safety Commission established and what is its motto?
2. State five functions of the FRSC
3. List five road safety rules enforced by the FRSC
4. Explain what guided composition means
5. Why is the Federal Road Safety Commission important to Nigeria?
ASSIGNMENT:
1. Write a guided composition (200-250 words) about "The Federal Road Safety
Commission" using the points provided in class
2. List ten traffic rules that every road user should obey
3. Design a simple road safety poster with at least three safety tips from FRSC
4. Explain five penalties for traffic offenses in Nigeria
5. Write five sentences about what you would do to improve road safety in your community

WEEK 3 - LITERATURE IN ENGLISH
TOPIC: POETRY – TYPES (EPIC, LYRIC, ETC.); ORAL VS WRITTEN POETRY
TYPES OF POETRY
Poetry can be classified into different types based on content, form, and purpose.
1. EPIC POETRY
Definition: Long narrative poems that tell heroic stories of legendary figures, nations, or
civilizations.
Features:
 Very long (hundreds or thousands of lines)
 Tells stories of heroes and their adventures
 Involves supernatural beings (gods, spirits)
 Covers important historical or legendary events
 Uses elevated/formal language
 Begins "in medias res" (in the middle of action)
 Contains lists, catalogs, and long speeches
 Has a serious tone
Examples:
 The Iliad and The Odyssey by Homer (Greek)
 Beowulf (English)
 The Divine Comedy by Dante (Italian)
 Paradise Lost by John Milton (English)
 African oral epics like Sundiata (Mali) and Ozidi (Nigeria)
Typical Epic Content:
384
 Battles and wars
 Journeys and quests
 Founding of nations
 Heroic deeds
 Cultural values and traditions
2. LYRIC POETRY
Definition: Short poems that express personal feelings, emotions, and thoughts of a single
speaker.
Features:
 Personal and subjective
 Expresses emotions (love, joy, sadness, anger)
 Usually short
 Musical quality
 First-person point of view (I, me, my)
 Focuses on feelings rather than storytelling
 Often has regular rhythm and rhyme
Types of Lyric Poetry:
a) Ode:
 Formal lyric poem
 Celebrates or praises a person, thing, or idea
 Serious and dignified tone
 Example: "Ode to a Nightingale" by John Keats
385
b) Sonnet:
 14-line poem
 Usually about love
 Specific rhyme scheme
 Example: Shakespearean sonnets
c) Elegy:
 Sad poem
 Mourns death or loss
 Reflective and melancholic
 Example: "Elegy Written in a Country Churchyard" by Thomas Gray
d) Song/Ballad:
 Meant to be sung
 Simple language
 Tells a story with emotion
3. NARRATIVE POETRY
Definition: Poetry that tells a story with characters, plot, and setting.
Features:
 Has a storyline
 Contains characters
 Has beginning, middle, and end
 Combines elements of poetry and prose
 May be long or short
386
Types:
 Ballads (folk stories in verse)
 Epic poetry (long narratives)
 Mock epic (humorous imitation of epic)
4. DRAMATIC POETRY
Definition: Poetry written in the voice of a character, often as a monologue or dialogue.
Features:
 Character speaks directly
 Reveals personality and situation
 Creates dramatic effect
 Often addresses someone
Types:
 Dramatic monologue
 Soliloquy
 Dialogue poems
5. DESCRIPTIVE POETRY
Definition: Poetry that vividly describes people, places, objects, or experiences.
Features:
 Rich in imagery
 Appeals to the five senses
 Creates clear mental pictures
387
 Uses figurative language heavily
 Focus on showing, not telling
6. FREE VERSE
Definition: Poetry without regular rhythm, rhyme, or structure.
Features:
 No fixed pattern
 Natural speech rhythm
 Modern and flexible
 Focuses on imagery and meaning
 Freedom in form
ORAL VS WRITTEN POETRY
ORAL POETRY
Definition: Poetry that is composed, performed, and transmitted by word of mouth, not written
down.
Characteristics:
1. Performance-Based:
 Meant to be spoken/sung aloud
 Uses voice, gestures, and facial expressions
 Interactive with audience
 Performed at gatherings, ceremonies, festivals
2. Memory-Based:
388
 Passed from generation to generation orally
 Memorized, not read
 May change over time as it's retold
 Uses repetition to aid memory
3. Cultural Context:
 Part of community traditions
 Performed at specific occasions (weddings, funerals, celebrations)
 Teaches cultural values and history
 Preserves heritage
4. Stylistic Features:
 Repetition of words, phrases, lines
 Call and response patterns
 Strong rhythm and rhyme
 Simple, memorable language
 Use of formulas and stock phrases
 Accompanied by music, drums, or dance
5. Types of Oral Poetry:
 Praise poems (oriki in Yoruba)
 Work songs
 War songs
 Lullabies
 Festival chants
 Funeral dirges
389
 Folk ballads
 Epic poems (like Ozidi)
Examples in African Context:
 Praise poetry for kings and warriors
 Moonlight tales with poetic elements
 Harvest songs
 Initiation chants
 Wedding songs
Functions of Oral Poetry:
 Entertainment
 Preservation of history
 Teaching moral lessons
 Expressing emotions
 Strengthening community bonds
 Celebrating achievements
 Honoring the dead
WRITTEN POETRY
Definition: Poetry that is composed in written form and transmitted through texts.
Characteristics:
1. Text-Based:
390
 Written down and published
 Read silently or aloud
 Permanent and fixed form
 Can be analyzed and studied
2. Individual Authorship:
 Created by known poets
 Original compositions
 Copyright protected
 Attributed to specific authors
3. Literary Context:
 Found in books, magazines, journals
 Studied in schools
 Part of literary tradition
 Subject to literary criticism
4. Stylistic Features:
 Complex language and imagery
 Sophisticated techniques
 Visual arrangement on page
 Line breaks and stanzas matter
 May use unusual punctuation
 Appeals to eye as well as ear
5. Types of Written Poetry:
 Sonnets, odes, elegies
391
 Free verse
 Haiku
 Concrete/shape poetry
 Modern experimental poetry
COMPARISON TABLE
Aspect Oral Poetry Written Poetry
Transmission Spoken/performed Written/printed
Authorship Often anonymous Known author
Preservation Memory Books/documents
Performance Live with audience Read individually
Change May vary over time Fixed text
Language Simple, repetitive Can be complex
Purpose Community function Artistic expression
Relationship Between Oral and Written:
 Many written poems draw from oral traditions
 Some oral poems have been written down
 Both use similar poetic devices
 Both express human experiences
 Modern poets often blend both traditions
African Context:
 Rich tradition of oral poetry
392
 Oral poetry predates written forms
 Many African languages have strong oral poetry traditions
 Contemporary African poets combine oral and written traditions
 Examples: Wole Soyinka, Chinua Achebe, Niyi Osundare use oral elements in written
poetry
EVALUATION:
1. Define epic poetry and state four of its features
2. Explain the difference between lyric poetry and narrative poetry with examples
3. What is oral poetry? List five characteristics of oral poetry
4. Compare oral poetry and written poetry, stating three differences
5. Name three types of lyric poetry and briefly describe each
ASSIGNMENT:
1. Explain four types of poetry studied in class with one example each
2. Write a short lyric poem (8 lines) expressing your feelings about school, nature, or
friendship
3. List five examples of oral poetry found in your culture or community
4. Explain five functions of oral poetry in African society
5. State five differences between oral and written poetry in a table format
