WEEK 10 - SPEECH WORK
TOPIC: CONTRASTING DIPHTHONGS /Eɪ/ VS /ɪƏ/ (DATE/HERE), (LATE/CHEER)
A diphthong is a vowel sound formed by the combination of two different vowel sounds within
one syllable. The tongue glides from one position to another while pronouncing it.
1. /eɪ/ Sound:
 Glide from /e/ to /ɪ/
 Found in words like: date, late, make, name, face, rain, play, day.
Examples in Sentences:
 I will play with my mate.
 The rain came late.
2. /ɪə/ Sound:
 Glide from /ɪ/ to /ə/
 Found in words like: here, cheer, fear, near, appear, career, idea.
Examples in Sentences:
 We sat near the window.
 She felt fear in the dark.
3. Contrasting the Two:
/eɪ/ /ɪə/
date dear
late leer
gate gear
male mere
642
fade fear
EVALUATION:
1. What is a diphthong?
2. Give three examples of words with /eɪ/.
3. Give three examples of words with /ɪə/.
4. Differentiate between /eɪ/ and /ɪə/.
5. Pronounce these pairs: date/dear, late/leer, play/peer.
ASSIGNMENT:
1. Write five word pairs contrasting /eɪ/ and /ɪə/.
2. Use three of them in sentences.
3. Record and listen to yourself pronouncing both sounds.
643
WEEK 10 - GRAMMAR
TOPIC: EXERCISE ON ADVERBIALS
1. Meaning of Adverbials
An adverbial is a word, phrase, or clause that gives more information about a verb, an adjective,
or another adverb. It tells us how, when, where, or why something happens.
Adverbials perform the function of adverbs in sentences, but unlike single-word adverbs,
adverbials can be phrases or clauses.
Examples:
 She sings beautifully. (adverb)
 She sings with great passion. (adverbial phrase)
 She sings because she is happy. (adverbial clause)
2. Types of Adverbials
There are four main types of adverbials according to the kind of information they give:
1. Adverbials of Time – tell when something happens.
o Examples: yesterday, last week, soon, in the morning, after dinner.
o Sentence examples:
 We shall travel tomorrow.
 He arrived in the evening.
2. Adverbials of Place – tell where something happens.
o Examples: here, there, everywhere, at home, in the market, to school.
o Sentence examples:
 The children are playing outside.
 She lives in Lagos.
3. Adverbials of Manner – tell how something happens.
644
o Examples: quickly, slowly, carefully, with joy, in anger.
o Sentence examples:
 The boy ran quickly.
 She spoke with confidence.
4. Adverbials of Reason or Purpose – tell why something happens.
o Examples: because of hunger, to get food, in order to learn.
o Sentence examples:
 He left early to catch the bus.
 She is crying because she lost her book.
Other subtypes include:
 Adverbials of Frequency: always, often, seldom, rarely, sometimes.
 Adverbials of Degree: very, quite, almost, too, rather.
3. Forms of Adverbials
Form Example Sentence
Single-word adverb quickly, slowly He ran quickly.
Adverbial phrase in the morning, with care She reads in the morning.
Adverbial clause because he was late He ran because he was late.
4. Position of Adverbials in Sentences
Adverbials can appear:
 At the beginning: Yesterday, we went to the zoo.
 In the middle: We usually eat rice on Sundays.
 At the end: She danced gracefully.
Notes:
645
 Adverbials of manner, place, and time often come at the end of sentences.
Example: She sang sweetly at the concert yesterday.
 When there is more than one adverbial, the usual order is:
Manner → Place → Time
Example: She danced gracefully (manner) in the hall (place) yesterday (time).
5. Common Errors with Adverbials
 Misplacing the adverbial in a sentence.
o ❌ He quickly speaks English.
o ✅ He speaks English quickly.
 Using double negatives with adverbials of frequency.
o ❌ He doesn’t go nowhere.
o ✅ He doesn’t go anywhere.
 Confusing manner and reason adverbials.
o ❌ She left carefully she was tired.
o ✅ She left because she was tired.
6. Exercises on Adverbials
A. Identify the adverbials and state their types:
1. She will return next week.
2. The dog slept under the table.
3. He smiled with joy.
4. They waited because the teacher was coming.
5. We go to church every Sunday.
B. Fill in the blanks with suitable adverbials:
646
1. He reads his Bible __________.
2. They are working __________ the classroom.
3. The meeting started __________.
4. The girl danced __________ the music stopped.
5. The children shouted __________ the teacher entered.
C. Combine these sentences using adverbials:
1. She cried. She lost her book.
→ She cried because she lost her book.
2. He walked. He was in a hurry.
→ He walked quickly.
3. The students are learning. They are in the library.
→ The students are learning in the library.
EVALUATION:
1. Define an adverbial.
2. State four main types of adverbials and give two examples of each.
3. Differentiate between an adverb and an adverbial.
4. Identify the adverbials in this sentence: The boy ran quickly to school yesterday.
5. Arrange the adverbials in correct order of manner, place, and time.
ASSIGNMENT:
1. Write ten sentences using at least one adverbial in each. Underline the adverbials.
2. Write three sentences using adverbial phrases and three using adverbial clauses.
3. Explain why correct placement of adverbials is important in English sentences.
647
WEEK 10 - READING
TOPIC: READING COMPREHENSION PRACTICE; VOCABULARY – SPELLING
DRILLS (CONTINUED)
1. Reading Comprehension Practice
Meaning of Comprehension:
Comprehension means understanding what is read. It is the ability to make meaning from
written text by identifying the main ideas, supporting details, and the writer’s intention. Reading
comprehension develops vocabulary, thinking ability, and general knowledge.
Purpose of Reading Comprehension:
 To understand the meaning of a text.
 To answer questions based on what is read.
 To develop the ability to infer or draw conclusions.
 To improve spelling, grammar, and general language use.
Skills Involved in Reading Comprehension:
1. Skimming: Reading quickly to get the general idea of a passage.
2. Scanning: Reading quickly to locate specific information.
3. Detailed Reading: Reading carefully to understand all ideas and information.
4. Inferring: Drawing conclusions or hidden meanings from what is read.
5. Summarizing: Giving a brief statement of the main points in a passage.
Steps in Answering Comprehension Questions:
1. Read the passage carefully at least twice.
648
2. Identify the main idea and key details.
3. Note unfamiliar words and their meanings from context.
4. Read the questions thoroughly and understand what is asked.
5. Answer in complete sentences, using your own words where possible.
6. Check for correct spelling, grammar, and punctuation.
Example Practice Passage (Excerpt):
The government of Nigeria has continued to encourage tree planting across the nation. Trees
help to prevent erosion, provide shade, and improve the quality of air. In schools and
communities, pupils are encouraged to plant and nurture trees as part of environmental
awareness campaigns.
Sample Questions:
1. What does the passage say about tree planting?
2. Mention two benefits of trees according to the passage.
3. Why does the government encourage pupils to plant trees?
4. Find the meaning of “nurture” as used in the passage.
5. Suggest a suitable title for this passage.
Tips for Effective Comprehension Reading:
 Read in a quiet environment.
 Avoid reading word by word—read in sense groups.
 Keep a dictionary nearby to learn new words.
649
 Read different types of texts: stories, articles, letters, and reports.
2. Vocabulary – Spelling Drills (Continued)
Meaning of Vocabulary:
Vocabulary refers to the total number of words a person knows and uses correctly in speech and
writing. A rich vocabulary helps students express themselves more clearly and understand texts
better.
Meaning of Spelling:
Spelling is the correct arrangement of letters in a word. Spelling drills are exercises that help
learners master the accurate spelling of words.
Importance of Good Spelling:
 It improves written communication.
 It prevents misunderstanding in writing.
 It increases confidence in reading and writing.
 It helps in word pronunciation and vocabulary building.
Commonly Misspelled Words (Revision and Continuation):
Wrong Spelling Correct Spelling Meaning
recieve receive to get something
occured occurred happened
enviroment environment surroundings
beleive believe to accept as true
goverment government system of ruling
definate definite certain, clear
seperate separate divide or set apart
650
accomodate accommodate to provide lodging
neccessary necessary required, needed
tommorow tomorrow the next day
Techniques for Improving Spelling:
1. Look, Say, Cover, Write, and Check Method:
o Look at the word carefully.
o Say it aloud.
o Cover it.
o Write it from memory.
o Check your spelling.
2. Use the word in a sentence to help remember its meaning.
3. Break words into syllables for easier memorization.
o Example: ac-com-mo-date, sep-a-rate.
4. Write difficult words repeatedly until correct spelling is mastered.
5. Keep a personal spelling book of words you find challenging.
Spelling Drill Practice:
 Write each of the following words correctly three times: environment, occurred,
government, necessary, believe.
 Use each of these words in a sentence of your own.
 Find two new words you often misspell and learn their correct spellings.
EVALUATION
651
1. Define comprehension and explain its purpose.
2. List and explain three skills involved in reading comprehension.
3. Write down three steps to follow when answering comprehension questions.
4. Explain what spelling means and why it is important.
5. Correct the following misspellings: recieve, goverment, seperate, definate, tommorow.
ASSIGNMENT
1. Read any short passage from your English textbook and summarize it in five sentences.
2. Write ten words you find difficult to spell correctly, and write their correct spellings.
3. Use five of the corrected words in meaningful sentences of your own.

WEEK 11 - SPEECH WORK
TOPIC: CONTRASTING /ƱƏ/ VS /OƱ/ (TOUR/POLE)
A diphthong is a combination of two vowel sounds pronounced together in one syllable, where
the tongue glides from one position to another. In this lesson, we focus on the contrast between
/ʊə/ and /oʊ/.
1. The /ʊə/ Sound:
 This sound begins with /ʊ/ and glides to /ə/.
 It is commonly found in words spelt with “our”, “oor”, “ure”, or “ure.”
 It occurs in British English, but in American English, /ʊə/ is often replaced with a pure
vowel /ɔː/ or /ʊr/.
Examples:
 tour /tʊə/
 sure /ʃʊə/
 poor /pʊə/
 cure /kjʊə/
 endure /ɪnˈdjʊə/
2. The /oʊ/ Sound:
 This sound begins with /o/ and glides to /ʊ/.
 It is heard in words with spellings like “o”, “oa”, “ow”, “oe”, “old.”
 It is common in both British and American English.
Examples:
 pole /poʊl/
 go /goʊ/
657
 home /hoʊm/
 boat /boʊt/
 window /ˈwɪndoʊ/
3. Contrast Between /ʊə/ and /oʊ/:
/ʊə/ /oʊ/
tour toe
poor pole
sure show
cure coat
pure pole
4. Pronunciation Tips:
 /ʊə/: The lips are rounded, then relaxed.
 /oʊ/: The lips start mid-rounded and become more rounded.
 Practice slowly, then increase fluency.
EVALUATION:
1. Define a diphthong.
2. Differentiate between /ʊə/ and /oʊ/ in pronunciation.
3. Give three examples of words with /ʊə/.
4. Give three examples of words with /oʊ/.
5. Pronounce these pairs: tour/toe, sure/show, cure/coat.
658
ASSIGNMENT:
1. Write five pairs of words contrasting /ʊə/ and /oʊ/.
2. Use three pairs in short sentences.
3. Record yourself pronouncing all examples correctly.
659
WEEK 11 - GRAMMAR
TOPIC: REVISION – NOUNS, PRONOUNS, VERBS, ADJECTIVES
This revision covers four key parts of speech previously taught.
1. Nouns:
A noun is a name of a person, place, animal, thing, or idea.
Types of Nouns:
 Proper Nouns: Names of specific people or places (Nigeria, Musa, Abuja).
 Common Nouns: General names (boy, city, car).
 Abstract Nouns: Names of ideas (love, truth, beauty).
 Collective Nouns: Names of groups (flock, team, class).
Examples in Sentences:
 Musa lives in Abuja. (Proper Noun)
 The team won the match. (Collective Noun)
2. Pronouns:
Pronouns are words used instead of nouns to avoid repetition.
Types:
 Personal Pronouns: I, you, he, she, it, we, they
 Possessive Pronouns: mine, yours, hers, ours, theirs
 Reflexive Pronouns: myself, himself, herself
 Relative Pronouns: who, which, that
Example:
 John said he was tired. (“he” replaces “John”)
660
3. Verbs:
A verb shows action or state of being.
 Action Verbs: run, jump, write
 Linking Verbs: is, seem, appear
 Auxiliary Verbs: have, do, will, can
Examples:
 She runs every morning.
 He is a teacher.
4. Adjectives:
Adjectives describe nouns or pronouns.
They tell quality, quantity, number, size, or color.
Examples:
 A tall man.
 Three girls came late.
 The red car is fast.
EVALUATION:
1. What is a noun? Give two examples.
2. Mention three types of pronouns.
3. State two kinds of verbs.
4. What is an adjective? Give two examples.
5. Identify the adjectives in this sentence: The old man bought three large bags.
661
ASSIGNMENT:
1. Write five sentences showing the use of nouns, pronouns, verbs, and adjectives.
2. Underline all verbs in your sentences.
3. Classify each noun in your sentences as common, proper, or abstract.

WEEK 11 - READING
TOPIC: READING FOR EVALUATION
1. Meaning of Reading for Evaluation:
Reading for evaluation means reading a passage critically to judge the ideas, arguments, and
opinions expressed. The reader must identify what is good, bad, or questionable in the text.
2. Purpose of Reading for Evaluation:
 To form opinions based on facts.
 To identify bias or exaggeration.
 To judge the logic or reasonableness of ideas.
3. Steps to Reading for Evaluation:
1. Read carefully and understand the author’s viewpoint.
2. Identify evidence or examples used.
3. Decide whether the argument is balanced or one-sided.
4. Form your own opinion and support it with reasons.
4. Example Passage (Excerpt):
"Television has both advantages and disadvantages. It educates, informs, and entertains.
However, too much television can lead to laziness and poor academic performance."
Sample Evaluation:
The writer presents both good and bad sides, showing a balanced argument. The passage is
reasonable because it warns against excessive watching while recognizing its benefits.
EVALUATION:
1. What is reading for evaluation?
2. Mention two purposes of reading for evaluation.
663
3. List three steps in evaluating a passage.
4. What is the author’s opinion in the passage about television?
5. Do you agree or disagree with the writer’s point? Why?
ASSIGNMENT:

or disagree with the writer.
2. State two benefits and two disadvantages of mobile phones.
3. Identify one example of bias in the passage, if any.

WEEK 11 - COMPOSITION
TOPIC: REVISION OF ALL ESSAY TYPES
1. Major Types of Essays:
 Narrative Essay: Tells a story.
Example: The Day I Got Lost.
 Descriptive Essay: Describes a person, place, or thing.
Example: My Best Friend.
 Expository Essay: Explains or gives information.
Example: How to Prepare for an Examination.
 Argumentative Essay: Argues for or against a point of view.
Example: Boarding Schools Are Better Than Day Schools.
2. Differences Between Essay Types:
Type Purpose Example
Narrative To tell a story My First Day in School
Descriptive To describe vividly My Village
Expository To explain How to Stay Healthy
Argumentative To convince Phones Should Be Banned in Class
3. Essay Writing Tips:
 Begin with a clear introduction.
 Use paragraphs for organization.
 Include examples or evidence.
 Conclude neatly.
 Check grammar and spelling.
665
EVALUATION:

WEEK 11 - LITERATURE IN ENGLISH
TOPIC: FIGURES OF SPEECH – EUPHEMISM AND PUN
1. Euphemism:
Euphemism is a mild or polite expression used in place of a harsh or unpleasant one. It helps to
soften sensitive messages.
Examples:
 He passed away (instead of He died).
 She is expecting a baby (instead of She is pregnant).
 They are economically disadvantaged (instead of They are poor).
Functions of Euphemism:
 To show respect and politeness.
 To reduce the emotional effect of unpleasant information.
 To maintain social harmony.
2. Pun:
A pun is a play on words that have similar sounds but different meanings, often used to create
humor or wit.
Examples:
 I used to be a banker but I lost interest.
 Time flies like an arrow; fruit flies like a banana.
 The tallest building in town is the library — it has the most stories.
Differences Between Euphemism and Pun:
Feature Euphemism Pun
Purpose To be polite or gentle To be humorous or witty
667
Example “He passed on.” “I lost interest (in banking).”
EVALUATION:
1. What is a euphemism?
2. Give three examples of euphemism.
3. What is a pun?
4. Give two examples of puns.
5. State one difference between euphemism and pun.
ASSIGNMENT:
1. Write five sentences using euphemisms.
2. Create three short jokes or puns.
3. Explain why writers use euphemisms and puns in literature.
